Pond to E. Brimfield Lake. No fee.
Mansfield Hollow Lake
141 Mansfield Hollow Rd.
Mansfield Center, CT 06250, 860-923-2982
nae.usace.army.mil/Missions/Recreation/
Mansfield-Hollow-Lake/
This U.S. Army Corps facility is leased and 
operated by the State of Connecticut for 
multifaceted outdoor enjoyment & activity. 
See Mansfield Hollow State Park for details. 
No fee.
Mansfield Hollow State Park
Bassetts Bridge Rd., Mansfield, CT 06250
860-928-6121, 860-424-3200
portal.ct.gov/DEEP/State-Parks/Parks/
Mansfield-Hollow-State-Park
The man-made, 450-acre lake is a big draw, 
with boat launch (8 mph limit), fishing & ice 
fishing, sailing & paddling. Trails for hiking, 
mountain biking, cross-country skiing & 
snowshoeing. Stroll & enjoy panoramic views 
from the dam. Open fields invite field sports 
& kite flying. Picnic tables, fire places & pit 
toilets. No fee.
Mashamoquet Brook State Park
147 Wolf Den Dr. (Rt. 44),  
omfret Center, CT 06259
860-928-6121, 
portal.ct.gov/DEEP/State-Parks/Parks/
Mashamoquet-Brook-State-Park 
3 parks in 1 (Mashamoquet, Wolf Den & 
Saptree Run) with trails for hiking. Exciting 
natural features: Table Rock, Indian Chair & 
General Israel Putnam’s Wolf Den. Springtime 
trout stocking of the brook will hook you on 
stream fishing. Enjoy swimming, camping (2 
areas), youth group camping, picnic tables & 
shelters, dump station & bathrooms. Some 
fees. 
Mohegan State Forest
Waldo Rd. (off Rt. 97), Scotland, CT 06264
860-424-3200, portal.ct.gov/deep
700 acres in 2 parcels (smaller piece in 
Sprague) make up this hardwood and 
softwood forest. Roadside parking by gate (no 
parking area), will lead you to gravel road for 
hiking, snowshoeing & cross-country skiing, 
horseback riding, letterboxing & old stone 
walls. Hunting in season. No fee.
Moosup Valley State Park Trail
Jct. Main St. & Rt. 14, Plainfield, CT 06354
portal.ct.gov/DEEP/State-Parks/Parks/
Moosup-Valley-State-Park-Trail
A 5.8-mile linear trail runs to Sterling with 
connection to Rhode Island. A former rail 
trail, you’ll traverse gravel to ballast surfaces. 
Glimpse the Moosup River & Sterling Pond in 
this rural setting. Good for beginners. No fee.
Mooween State Park
Old Rt. 2 to Camp Mooween Rd.,  
Lebanon, CT 06249,  
860-424-3200, portal.ct.gov/deep
On the eastern side of Red Cedar Lake, this 
577-acre park was a former boys camp. See 
chimneys, stairs & other camp relics returning 
to nature, as well remains of the 300 year old 
Granny Austin Tavern. Trails for hiking, while 
lake welcomes boating, paddling & fishing. 
Hunting in season. No fee.
155

PAGE 156
State & Federal Parks & Forests
Natchaug State Forest
66 Pilfershire Rd. (Rt. 198), Eastford, CT 06242
860-928-6121, 860-424-3200
portal.ct.gov/DEEP/State-Parks/Forests/
Natchaug-State-Forest
13,200-acre park spreads over 6 towns. Year-
round activities include camping (backpack 
& horse), horseback riding, hiking, mountain 
biking, letterboxing, cross-country skiing, 
snowshoeing, snowmobiling & hunting. 
Historic site of Gen. Nathaniel Lyon’s birthplace 
remains. Rivers & ponds offer varied paddles, 
fishing & riverside picnic sites. Pit toilets. No fee.
Nathan Hale State Forest
Off Case Rd., Coventry, CT 06238
860-424-3200, portal.ct.gov/deep
1,500-acre forest of hemlock, white cedar, 
American chestnut restoration sites, and grass 
& shrub fields. Perfect for hiking/strolling, 
mountain biking, snowshoeing/cross-country 
skiing or letterboxing. Historic Nathan Hale 
Homestead close by to extend your visit. 
Wildlife viewing is ideal, hunting allowed in 
season. No fee.
Nipmuck State Forest
Rt. 171 & Rt. 190, Union, CT 06076
860-684-3430, 860-424-3200
portal.ct.gov/deep
Combined with Bigelow Hollow State Park 
there are 9,000+ acres with many hiking trails 
maintained by CT Forest & Park Association. 
3 bodies of water range from 20+ to 300 
acres and offer fishing, boating (launches) & 
scuba diving. Hunting, cross-country skiing, 
snowshoeing & snowmobiling, backpack 
camping, plus picnic tables & pit toilets. 
Mountain Laurel Sanctuary (CT State Flower) 
greets you at Rt. 190 entrance. Some fees.
Old Furnace State Park
So. Frontage Rd./Ross Rd. (off Rt. 6),  
Killingly, CT 06239
860-424-3200, portal.ct.gov/deep
367-acre park was home to 18th-century 
foundry, a major supplier of horseshoes to 
Continental Army in revolutionary war. Hikers 
are rewarded with 10 mile wide panoramic 
views after a 0.75 mile hike rising 200’ up. Loop 
trail is 4 miles. 31-acre lake has car-top/carry-in 
boat launch, paddling & fishing. Hunting in 
season. No fee.
Pachaug State Forest
Rt. 49, Voluntown, CT 06384
860-376-4075, portal.ct.gov/DEEP/State-
Parks/Forests/Pachaug-State-Forest
Exceptional activity selection in 26,000 acres 
with miles of roads & trails in CT’s largest 
state forest. Boating (launches), fishing 
(accessible platform), paddling, swimming, 
various camping types, fireplaces, pit toilets, 
horseback riding, hiking, letterboxing, 
picnicking (tables), mountain biking, 
motorcycles, dog sled races, hunting, cross-
country skiing, snowshoeing, snowmobiling, 
scenic vistas including 441’ Mt. Misery 
overlook accessible by trail or car & 26-acre 
rhododendron sanctuary ablaze around July 
4th. Some fees.
Pomeroy State Park
Rt. 289, Lebanon, CT 06249
860-424-3200, portal.ct.gov/deep
This undeveloped and unmarked 200-acre 
park, part of which was textile manufacturer 
Charles Pomeroy’s estate, has limited roadside 
parking. Hike along the top of Bush Hill. 
Hunting. No fee.
Quaddick State Park and Forest
818 Town Farm Rd., Thompson, CT 06277
860-928-6121, 860-424-3200
portal.ct.gov/deep
Park with 400+ acre reservoir is a water lover’s 
delight. Boating (no restrictions & launch), 
fishing, paddling, swimming, restrooms 
& changing areas, ice fishing & skating, 
picnicking (tables & shelters), field sports, 
hiking. The mostly undeveloped Forest is 
north of the Park with hunting & fishing, 
letterboxing & camping. Some fees for Park. 
No fee at Forest.
Quinebaug Lake State Park
Shepard Hill Rd. (off Rt. 12), Killingly, CT 
06239
860-424-3200, portal.ct.gov/deep
This 181-acre park sparkles with exceptionally 
clear waters (also known as Wauregan 
Reservoir). Fresh water fishing opportunities 
with many varieties to catch. DEEP stocks 
brown & rainbow trout occasionally. Car-top 
boat ramp (electric motor only) and easy 
paddling, too. No fee.

PAGE 157
State & Federal Parks & Forests
Quinebaug Valley  
State Trout Hatchery
141 Trout Hatchery Rd., Central Village, CT 06332
860-564-7542, 
portal.ct.gov/DEEP/Fishing/Fisheries-
Management/Quinebaug-Hatchery
One of the largest state hatcheries sits on this 
2,000 acres. Starting with the hatch house, 
different tanks carry trout through their 
stages of life, supplying 700,000 brookies, 
browns & rainbows annually. Glass wall for 
viewing, self-guided tours available. Special 
children’s fishing pond is stocked often. Ponds 
& trails spread out to ensure easy walking & 
views of birds, turtles & more. Daily 8:30 a.m. - 
3:30 p.m. No fee.
Ross Pond State Park
Ross Rd., Killingly, CT 06239
860-424-3200, portal.ct.gov/deep
314-acre park has two ponds: smaller 
northern is Upper Ross (or Half Hill Pond), 
larger southern Ross Pond has boat ramp, 
frequently stocked for trout fishing from 
small boat, & easy paddling spot. Hiking on 
Old Furnace Trail running on western side of 
ponds. Hunting, outhouses. No fee.
Salt Rock State Campground
173 Scotland Rd. (Rt. 97), Sprague, CT 06330
860-424-3200, 860-822-0884
portal.ct.gov/DEEP/State-Parks/
Campgrounds/Salt-Rock-State-Campground
149 wooded acres offers many modern 
camping amenities (bathrooms, dump station, 
water, fire rings, picnic tables, playground & 
in-ground pool), with access to Shetucket 
River for fishing & room to stroll. No fees to 
visit. Camping fees apply.
Streeter Point Recreation Area
6 Streeter Point Ave., Sturbridge, MA 01566
508-347-9316, mass.gov
Paddling, fishing & boating with access to 
400 acre E. Brimfield Reservoir on U.S. Army 
Corps of Engineers ramps on Rt. 20. See East 
Brimfield Lake listing. No fees on U.S. Army 
Corps operated sites.
Wells State Park
159 Walker Pond Rd. (Rt. 49)
Sturbridge, MA 01566
508-347-9257, 877-422-6762 (camp 
reservations)
mass.gov/orgs/department-of-conservation-
recreation
1,400 wooded acres known best for hiking 
(10 miles of trails), camping (interpretive 
programs, guided hikes, recreational activities, 
evening campfires, swimming, dump station, 
pavillion) and some great views. Other 
activities are horseback riding, mountain 
biking, playing fields, basketball court, 
hunting, cross-country skiing & snowshoeing. 
Walker Pond has boat launch, fishing, 
paddling. Some fees.
West Thompson Lake
449 Reardon Rd., N. Grosvenordale, CT 06255
860-923-2982, , nae.usace.army.mil/Missions/
Recreation/West-Thompson-Lake/
Great variety of wildlife & activity in 1,125 
forested acres, 80 acres of open grasslands 
& 200-acre lake. Camping (horseshoes, 
basketball courts, private sites, dump 
station, firewood, accessible toilet/shower, 
interpretive programs, some water & 
electricity), hiking (17+ miles of trails) and 
water (paddle the lake & Quinebaug River 
National Recreation Water Trail, boat launch, 
fishing - trout stocked & freshwater fish 
aplenty) are popular. Disc golf, picnic shelters, 
hunting, snowmobiling, cross country skiing & 
snowshoeing. Some fees.
Westville Lake Recreation Area
125 Wallace Rd., Sturbridge, MA 01566
508-347-3705, mass.gov/orgs/department-of-
conservation-recreation 
578 acres of diverse land, 23-acre lake, plus 
access to Quinebaug River & Grand Trunk 
Trail. Boat launches for boating, fishing (warm 
water bass & stocked trout), paddling & ice 
skating when safe. 3 miles of walking trails 
range from flat to rolling hills, also great for 
snowshoeing, cross-country skiing & sledding. 
Sports fields, shelters, picnic tables & grills 
make for year-round enjoyment. Some fees.

From competition-ready swimming pools to nature’s scenic swimming holes, The Last Green 
Valley is home to wonderful swimming and scuba diving opportunities. Since many locations 
are ponds, lakes and streams, be sure to contact the location before making a trek as weather 
and rainfall can greatly affect the availability of summer swimming!
Swimming & Scuba Diving
Explore! OUTDOOR ACTIVITIES
SWIMMING/SPLASH PADS
Andrew J. Petro Memorial Swimming 
Pool/Southbridge State Pool
15 Randolph St., Southbridge, MA 01550
508-764-7352, facebook.com/AJPetroPool
Operated by the Town of Southbridge, this 
outdoor pool is open seasonally. Season 
or day passes available. Please check for 
availabilty. Some fees
Big Alum Pond
Clark Rd., Sturbridge, MA 01566
617-626-1250, mass.gov/eea
195-acre pond, 23-45’ deep, clear waters. 
Boat launch available, not much shore 
access (pond surrounded by private homes). 
Swimming & waterskiing are allowed here, as 
well as fishing. No fee.
Brimfield State Forest/ 
Dean Pond Recreation Area
86 Dearth Hill Rd., Brimfield, MA 01010
413-267-9687  
mass.gov/locations/brimfield-state-forest 
Dean Pond Recreation Area, located in the 
western portion of Brimfield State Forest (off 
Rt. 20) has 100 feet of beach and swimming. A 
pavilion, comfort station and picnic facilities 
also on site. Some fees.
Buffumville Lake
48 Old Oxford Rd. (off Buffum Dam Rd. & 
Charlton St.), Charlton, MA 01507 
508-248-5697, nae.usace.army.mil/Missions/
Recreation/Buffumville-Lake/
The park has a 300-foot beach with an 
enclosed swimming area for safety. 12 and 
older pay minimal fee for using the beach. 
Swimming, wading, scuba diving at your own 
risk. Some fees.
East Brimfield Lake/Streeter Beach
6 Streeter Point Ave., Sturbridge, MA 01566
508-347-9316, mass.gov
Swimming is available some years at Streeter 
Beach. The fee area is operated by the MA 
Dept. of Conservation and Recreation.  
Some fees. 
East Brookfield Town Beach –  
Lake Lashaway Beach
Rt. 9, East Brookfield, MA 01515
508-867-3832, eastbrookfieldma.us
The East Brookfield Parks and Recreation 
Committee maintains, along with help from 
North Brookfield, the Town Beach at Lake 
Lashaway. The beach opens in late June and 
closes for the year after Labor Day. Check 
website for details and fees.

Swimming & Scuba Diving
Finn Beach
61 North Shore Rd., Voluntown, CT 06384
860-376-4089, Voluntown.gov
A public, swim-at-your-own-risk beach open 
to anyone regardless of whether they live in 
Voluntown. Parking permits required for a 
fee & available at Town Clerk’s Office (free to 
Voluntown residents). No parking on streets, 
no dogs on beach. Check website for details 
and fees.
Hale YMCA Y outh & Family Center – 
Hartford  
9 Technology Park Dr., Putnam, CT 06260 
860-315-9622, haley@ghymca.org, haleYMCA.org 
Holland Pond/Lake Siog Recreation Area
Off Dug Hill Rd., Holland, MA 01521
508-347-3705, nae.usace.army.mil/Missions/
Recreation/East-Brimfield-Lake/
Public swimming and scuba diving at your 
own risk are allowed at Holland Pond/Lake 
Siog Park, which is operated by the U.S. Army 
Corps of Engineers. No fee.
Hopeville Pond State Park
929 Hopeville Rd. (Rt. 201), Griswold, CT 06351
860-376-2920, 860-376-0313
portal.ct.gov/DEEP/State-Parks/Parks/
Hopeville-Pond-State-Park
The 137-acre lake offers swimming as well as 
boating. Restrooms on site. Main gate open 
seasonally. Some fees.
Hygeia Reservoir
Off Valley Rd. (off Rt. 101), Killingly, CT 06239
860-779-5390, Killingly.org
Since 2015 the town-owned Hygeia Reservoir 
had been a swim-at-your-own-risk swimming 
area, meaning there is no lifeguard on duty. 
The 80-foot by 40-foot roped-off swimming 
area is fronted by a narrow strip of beach 
that includes a shaded tent near a portable 
bathroom. Open to residents of Killingly. Call 
Killingly Parks and Recreation Department for 
details. No fee.
Lebanon Community Center Pool
872 Trumbull Highway, Lebanon, CT 06249
(860) 642-6100
lebanonct.gov/community-center-pool 
The Community Center Pool is open each 
summer to residents of Lebanon, Franklin 
and their guests. The Lebanon Recreation 
Department uses this pool to offer swim 
lessons as well as for daily open swim. The 
pool has a shallow end suitable for kids and a 
deep end with a maximum depth of ten feet. 
Passes are required and can be purchased in 
town hall. The pool is also available for rental. 
Lauter Park Splash Pad
625 Jackson St., Willimantic, CT 06226
860-465-3046, windhamrec.org
Perfect for children, you will have fun keeping 
cool in the splash pad waters. The park also 
has a beach area along the Natchaug River. 
No fees.
Mansfield Community Center
10 South Eagleville Rd., Mansfield, CT 06268
860-429-3015, mansfieldct.gov
A 6-lane, 25-yard lap pool with diving area 
& heated mid-size therapy pool. A range of 
activities/programs is offered for all ages 
& abilities including lessons, water fitness 
classes, general recreational & lap swimming 
& more. Certified lifeguards. Special events 
rental available. Resident and non-resident 
fees apply.
Mashamoquet Brook State Park
147 Wolf Den Dr., (Rt. 44)
Pomfret Center, CT 06259
860-928-6121, portal.ct.gov/DEEP/State-
Parks/Parks/Mashamoquet-Brook-State-Park
The dammed brook in this park provides 
a small child-friendly swimming area. 
Depending on rainfall amounts as summer 
progresses, swimming is not always possible. 
Some fees.
Mohegan Park/Spaulding Pond
Park Center Rd./Mohegan Park Rd.
Norwich, CT 06360, 860-823-3798, norwichct.org
Swimming is allowed in designated areas only 
in Spaulding Pond. There is a beach area near 
one of the playgrounds and lifeguards are on 
duty. To help keep the waters clean, please do 
not feed the ducks & geese. Lakeside pavilion 
can be rented for events. Some fees.

SCUBA DIVING
Bigelow Hollow State Park
Rt. 171, Union, CT 06076
860-684-3430, 860-424-3200
portal.ct.gov/DEEP/State-Parks/Parks/
Bigelow-Hollow-State-Park-Nipmuck-State-
Forest
A rare spot to scuba dive in The Last Green 
Valley, Bigelow Hollow has 3 popular ponds 
ranging from 18 acres to 300 acres. Rock 
formations are great for an underwater 
glimpse of turtles, fish and more. As a popular 
hiking & fishing area, parking lots can fill up 
fast. Some fees.
Buffumville Lake
48 Old Oxford Rd. (off Buffum Dam Rd. &
Charlton St.), Charlton, MA 01507
508-248-5697, nae.usace.army.mil/Missions/
Recreation/Buffumville-Lake/
The park has a 300 foot long beach with an 
enclosed swimming area for safety. People 
over age 12 pay minimal fee for using the 
beach. Swimming, wading, scuba diving at 
your own risk. Some fees.
Holland Pond/ 
Lake Siog Recreation Area
off Dug Hill Rd., Holland, MA 01521

Recreation/East-Brimfield-Lake/
Swimimng and scuba diving at your own

Nipmuck State Forest
Rt. 171 & Rt. 190, Union, CT 06076
860-684-3430, 860-424-3200
portal.ct.gov/DEEP/State-Parks/Forests/
Nipmuck-State-Forest-Bigelow-Hollow- 
State-Park

PAGE 174
There’s no need to be house-bound in The Last Green Valley during winter. Whether by land 
or water, there are wonderful opportunities to get outside and Explore all winter. Please note, 
sledding locations vary dramatically from winter-to-winter based on many factors. 
Winter Activities
Explore! OUTDOOR ACTIVITIES
LAND/TRAILS
(snowshoeing, cross-country skiing 
and sledding)
You can often enjoy cross-country skiing and 
snowshoeing in the same areas. Snowshoers 
can generally traverse most hiking trails. Both 
activities are usually allowed on all multi-use 
trails and roads in state parks and forests. 
Trails are not machine-set or groomed, are  
not patrolled and rescue services are not 
provided.
Air Line State Park Trail
Lebanon, Windham, Chaplin, Hampton,
Pomfret, Putnam, Thompson, CT
860-295-9523, portal.ct.gov/deep
The Air Line Trail is a flat, linear, long trail, 
outstanding for easy cross-country ski or 
snowshoe fun. No motorized vehicles allowed 
(including snowmobiles). See website for 
parking info. No fee.
The Beeches Recreation Park
Senexet Village Rd., Woodstock, CT 06281
860-928-6595, woodstockconservation.org
Entrance to trail is near the basketball court 
on the west side of the soccer field. Loop 
trail is about 0.87 miles, flat or gently sloping 
except for 2 sections with slight grade. Wraps 
around a residential development, a shady 
walk with pretty views of Little River. Good for 
cross-county skiing or snowshoeing if there is 
snow. No fee.
Bigelow Hollow State Park/ 
Nipmuck State Forest
Rt. 171, Union, CT 06076
860-684-3430, 860-424-3200,  
portal.ct.gov/deep
Multi-use trails of various length & difficulty 
are available in the 9,000+ acre area for 
snowshoeing & cross-country skiing. 
Snowmobile trail is 9 miles. Some fees.
    A. Ranta

Winter Activities
Brimfield State Forest
86 Dearth Hill Rd., Brimfield, MA 01010
413-267-9687, mass.gov
Rolling, heavily forested property has more 
than 20 miles of roads and trails providing 
winter outdoor activities like snowshoeing & 
cross-country skiing. Some fees.
Brooklyn Elementary School
119 Gorman Rd., Brooklyn, CT 06234
Get in some sledding when school is 
cancelled or on winter weekends when no 
other events are taking place at the school. 
Be safe and respectful of this public property. 
No fee.
Chase Reservoir
85 Pratt Rd., East Killingly, CT 06243
860-774-5310, 860-234-0196
killinglyconservation.org, killingly.org
Choose either a 3.5 or 5-mile woodland trail 
through varied 285 acres with heavily wooded 
forests, historic foundations & wildlife habitat 
for cross-country skiing or snowshoeing. No 
horses or motorized vehicles. No fee.
Claire Birtz Wildlife Sanctuary - 
Opacum Land Trust  
Tipton Rock Rd. (off Rt. 169)
Southbridge, MA 01550, 508-347-9144
info@opacumlt.org, opacumlt.org
Park in small lot on road. Three snowshoe 
trails available (0.64, 0.5 and 0.41 miles) along 
the shore of Morse Pond, plus ice skating. 
Enjoy stands of beech trees, hemlock and 
mixed forest habitats, and old stone walls. 
No fee.
Hampton Elementary School
380 Main St., Hampton, CT 06247
Sledding is great fun when school is cancelled 
or on winter weekends when no other events 
are taking place at the school. Be safe and 
respectful of this public property. No fee.
Hodges Village Dam
30 Howarth Rd. (off Charlton Rd.)
Oxford, MA 01540, 508-248-5697
nae.usace.army.mil/missions/recreation 
20+ miles of trails for year-round use include 
cross-country skiing & snowshoeing for 
beating the winter blues. Off-road vehicle 
trails allow snowmobiles. No fee.
James L. Goodwin State Forest
23 Potter Rd. (off Rt. 6), Hampton, CT 06247
860-455-9534, portal.ct.gov/deep
Ten miles of wooded trails as well as views of 
Pine Acres Lake make for great snowshoeing 
& cross-country skiing. Open year round.  
No fee.
King St. Hill
King St., Putnam, CT 06260
860-963-6800, putnamct.us
Street parking only. King St. is a short 
residential street off Grove Street in Putnam 
with a nice town-owned sledding hill that has 
been frequented by local sledders for many 
years. Parking is tight and on the street. Be 
respectful of the homeowners in this area 
when you visit. No fee.
Mansfield Hollow State Park
Bassetts Bridge Rd., Mansfield, CT 06250
860-928-6121, 860-424-3200,  
portal.ct.gov/deep
There are several easy trails available for 
snowshoeing & cross-country skiing, some 
with spectacular views of Mansfield Hollow 
Lake. No fee.
Mohegan State Forest
Waldo Rd. (off Rt. 97), Scotland, CT 06264
860-424-3200, portal.ct.gov/deep
700 acres in 2 parcels (smaller piece in 
Sprague) make up this hardwood and 
softwood forest. Roadside parking by gate (no 
parking area), will lead you to gravel road for 
snowshoeing & cross-country skiing with nice 
old stone walls to view. No fee.
Natchaug State Forest
66 Pilfershire Rd. (Rt. 198), Eastford, CT 06242
860-928-6121, 860-424-3200,  
portal.ct.gov/deep
Expand your visiting season here by using the 
many trails over and through various terrain 
on snowshoes or cross-country skis, some 
more challenging than others. 17 miles of 
snowmobile trails. Pit toilets. No fee.

Winter Activities
Nathan Hale State Forest
off Case Rd., Coventry, CT 06238
860-424-3200, portal.ct.gov/deep
1,500-acre forest of hemlock, white cedar, 
American chestnut restoration sites and fields. 
Perfect for snowshoeing and cross-country 
skiing. Wildlife viewing is ideal, hunting 
allowed in season. No fee.
Pachaug State Forest
Rt. 49, Voluntown, CT 06384
860-376-4075, portal.ct.gov/deep
Explore many miles of roads & trails on 
snowshoes or cross-country skis for a chance 
to view CT’s largest state forest in the winter. 
The snowmobile trail utilizes unplowed forest 
roads & trails. Dog sled mushing competitions 
held here, too! Some fees.
Quinebaug Woods
Dug Hill Rd., Holland, MA 01521
413-245-7108, town.holland.ma.us
Parts of the moderate/short 1.1-mile loop trail 
are suitable for both snowshoeing and cross-
country skiing if there is snow. Follow the 
Quinebaug River. Head up and over a steep 
hillside with rocky outcrops if conditions 
allow. Mixed open canopy along the top of 
the “hogback” ridge, hemlocks dominate 
below, forming dense stands. View remains 
from a cabin built in 1932. Look over Blake 
Hill to the east and Hamilton Reservoir to the 
south. No fee.
Sayles School
25 Scotland Rd. (Rt. 97)
Baltic (Sprague), CT 06330
There is a little hill at this school, great for 
sledding when school is cancelled or on 
winter weekends when no other events are 
taking place at the school. Parking lot next to 
school. Be safe and respectful of this public 
property. No fee.
UConn Horsebarn Hill
Horsebarn Hill Rd., Storrs, CT 06269
860-486-2413, sustainability.uconn.edu/
development/parks.html
Climb up Horsebarn Hill for views of the 
UConn Storrs Campus & turn to view the 
forests in the other direction. Then hop on 
your sled and zoom down the hill! At your 
own risk, be mindful of the fencing at the 
bottom of the hill and any passing cars.  
No fee.
Voluntown Elementary School
195 Main St., Voluntown, CT 06384
A hill adjacent to the school makes for fun 
sledding for smaller kids when school is 
cancelled or on winter weekends when no 
other events are taking place at the school. 
Parking available. Be safe and respectful of 
this public property. No fee.
Wells State Park
159 Walker Pond Rd. (Rt. 49)
Sturbridge, MA 01566, 508-347-9257,  
mass.gov
A popular 1,400-acre woodland park with 
over 10 miles of trails to enjoy all winter long 
on your snowshoes & cross-country skis. 
Carpenter’s Rock Trail offers a view! Some fees.
West Thompson Lake
449 Reardon Rd., N. Grosvenordale, CT 06255
860-923-2982
nae.usace.army.mil/missions/recreation

nae.usace.army.mil/missions/recreation

Winter Activities
WATER/ICE
(ice skating, ice fishing)
Where you can ice fish, you can generally ice 
skate. Always be sure to confirm the safety 
and thickness of ice before engaging in 
these activities. Local fishing licenses/rules/ 
regulations apply.
East Brimfield Lake
24 Riverview Ave. (off Rt. 20), Fiskdale
(Sturbridge), MA 01518, 508-347-3705
nae.usace.army.mil/missions/recreation
Excellent ice fishing, featuring northern pike, 
pickerel, and bass. No fee.
Hodges Village Dam
30 Howarth Rd. (off Charlton Rd.)
Oxford, MA 01540, 508-248-5697
nae.usace.army.mil/missions/recreation
If you enjoy cold weather and winter sports, 
this is the spot for ice fishing at Augutteback 
Pond. No fee.
Lake Lashaway
Harrington St. (off Rt. 9), E. Brookfield/N.
Brookfield line, MA, 508-835-3607
mass.gov/wildlife or eastbrookfieldma.us
293-acre pond, transparent & average depth 
to 10’ with a maximum of 18’ , with a very 
developed shoreline. Pickerel here make 
for good ice fishing, and ice skating is great 
winter fun too. No fee.
Lake Siog Recreation Area/ 
Holland Pond

508-347-3705
nae.usace.army.mil/missions/recreation

Lebanon Town Green
Rt. 87 (near Rt. 207 intersection)
Lebanon, CT 06249

JOIN – DONATE – VOLUNTEER
The Last Green Valley National Heritage 
Corridor is the last stretch of dark night 
sky in the coastal sprawl between Boston 
and Washington, D.C. 
The Last Green Valley, Inc. works for you  
in the National Heritage Corridor.  
A member-supported, nonprofit 
charitable organization, we help you to 
care for it, enjoy it and pass it on! 
Fill out and mail the form below or join 
online at TheLastGreenValley.org.  
Enjoy the benefits of membership no 
matter what your circumstances are with 
our Pay-What-You-Can option. 
Benefits of Membership Include:
• Invitations to monthly member-only 
programs
• E-Newsletters and communications
• Publications like “In Touch”  
and Walktober
• Privilege to participate and vote  
in TLGV Committees, Board and  
Annual Meeting
• 10% discount for TLGV Online Shop
• Ability to become a TLGV Ranger
• Family Membership includes all of the 
above plus Acorn Club Membership  
for kids 12 & under.
